# NEXUS: test plan

[한국어](nexus.ko.md)

Stage 12 of CORTHEX’s own project. Status: execution in progress; all717 cases have scoped verdicts (692 pass,7 partial,18 not applicable,0 fail). Partial clauses remain unproved; adjudication is not full acceptance. Actual runs and original performance failures are retained below. English is the source and the Notion case database follows it. Inputs are stages 02–09, especially each active SPEC acceptance criterion, named OpenAPI response, hub command outcome and screen state. Phase-② editing/workshop flows FL-115/FL-116 are deferred with SPEC-134/SPEC-142.

## How to read this file

`ID` is permanent, starting at TC-601; BUG starts at BUG-501. `From` is the acceptance criterion, API response, SCR state or REQ under test. `Task` writes/runs the case in its finishing PR. `Runs as` names the actual method. `Steps` describes the fixture/action, `Expected` includes the response and effects and `Last result` names environment, commit and evidence when run. not run means not run. A response example checked only with Prism is counted separately from development evidence.

For0.2.1, not applicable means that an exact legacy shared example cannot occur under that operation's declared input or role. It is excluded from passing evidence; its original row and verdict remain in the versioned applicability proof. Seven corrected endpoint-specific examples have new IDs TC-1311–1317.

## Where the cases run

- Server: Node built-in tests with in-memory SQLite/D1, Wrangler local D1, then corthex-nexus-dev behind Access. Contract tools live outside the repository: openapi-spec-validator 0.7.1, Prism 5.16.0, Schemathesis 3.39.16 with experimental OpenAPI 3.1.
- Hub: standard-library unittest, isolated home/project and fake local HTTP; Python 3.8 and latest on Linux/macOS/Windows, including real Git object fixtures.
- Views: Prism first, then the real development Worker; browser agents use Aside CLI where available, otherwise a named host browser tester. Start three isolated browser sessions and measure safe concurrency before increasing. No production writes.
- Every state: 320/1440 pixels, light/dark and English/Korean; page-check.js measurements and separately inspected screenshots.
- Credentials remain in this host’s secret store/environment. Access evidence records presence/empty only. Synthetic data is seeded per role and tester; this repository is never pushed without REQ-107 consent.

## Kinds of cases

| Kind | Coverage or reason |
| --- | --- |
| Access | Both real identities, wrong audience/kind, missing/expired sign-in, cross-site writes; refusal changes nothing. |
| Input | Required/blank/long/invalid/unknown fields, malformed UTF-8/JSON and direct API requests, plus CLI validation. |
| Target | Unknown/malformed/deleted project, part, picture, item and entry; never an accidental 500. |
| State changes | Reopen/settle/decide, proposal transitions, reflection reports, checkpoint withdrawal and recorded-state warnings. |
| Repeats | Lost replies, double writes, same ID and another request, repeated reports and seven-day expiry. |
| Concurrency | Interleaved pushes, two sessions/claims, simultaneous quota use, two hubs on one outbox and two part records. |
| Limits | Snapshot/body/text sizes, 30 projects, space text, open entries, bounded list totals and free CPU/query budgets. |
| Outside services | Network/timeouts/token refusal; editors, fonts and assets stay on the same Access-protected origin, served by the approved standalone runtime (or the separately tested Worker compatibility runtime); no external runtime service. |
| Data exposure | No identities except roles; no home paths/secrets; full proposal source only on own read; XSS remains text. |
| Empty and streamed answers | Empty hub, no picture/entries/to-do/outbox, HEAD no body; no streaming API is specified. |
| Screen states | Each declared state above at both widths/themes/languages; hash panels, drafts and editor fallback. |
| AI answers | Not applicable: NEXUS calls no AI model (REQ-121); no paid call is made. |

## Performance

Current REQ-124/TASK-223 disposition: pass under the approved 2026-11-04 standalone definition, as recorded in TC-1270. The exact synthetic maximum (1,000,000 bytes, 3,000 items, 2,000 checks, three picture sources) took 31.085 ms after complete body receipt, below 1 s. Keep inclusive processing 1031.079 ms, body receipt 999.994 ms, round trip 26982.324 ms, CPU-window 74.077 ms and post-response RSS 139616256 bytes as separate observations. The earlier free-Worker CPU trial failed at 83 ms against its 10 ms budget; its query/row evidence and contemporaneous pending decision remain historical entries below.

Current REQ-149/TASK-224 disposition: pass for the first complete SVG under the maintainer's SVG-first decision. The [formal v8 study](runs/2026-11-05-svg-mobile-v8.md) qualified all60 openings,20 per 300-element picture, under the unchanged390×844/DPR2.75/touch/Fast4G/cache-off/CPU1 profile. Nearest-rank P95 (rank19 of20) was DDD1399.2 ms, ERD1327.2 ms and sitemap1357.3 ms, each below3000 ms. Native readiness/paint, additional CPU4 stress, actual code/font bytes and CSP/self-origin checks retain their separate recorded scope. Earlier incomplete403 openings, native-only timing failures and v5's520 response remain preserved with their original denominators and verdicts; see the [operating notes](../../nexus/README.md#preserved-v5-code-response-failure).

## Non-functional requirements

REQ-068/076/077: object-only reading, snapshot allowlisted content, redaction/no paths (TASK-225–TASK-230). REQ-082/083: Python 3.8/latest and three OSs (TASK-250). REQ-097/098/101 and REQ-146–REQ-148: offline hub, responsive/token UI, same-origin code and text-only rendering (TASK-237–TASK-243, TASK-251). REQ-109/110: environment-only credentials and secret-free queues/logs (TASK-233–TASK-235). REQ-113/114/119: Access/custom host/security headers/safe logs (TASK-201, TASK-209, TASK-210, TASK-222). REQ-116/118/123: quotas, minimal retention and all SQL in store.js (TASK-203–TASK-221). REQ-121/122: no AI/Git rights on server (code/security review). REQ-124/149: measured above. REQ-126: bilingual deployment/upgrade guide (TASK-247).

## Walking the flows

TASK-251 builds one run row per FL-101–FL-114 main/failure path and actual role, using the phase-① rows of each flow. Each tester gets isolated seed data and its own tabs; it records visible text/screenshots after each action and verifies expected data effects through the API/read-only query. Reports name commit, tool/version, widths, role/path, TC, works/fails/blocked, evidence and impressions separately. Working paths become e2e CI tests. FL-115 and FL-116 are not run: deferred phase ②.

## The release decision

The maintainer's 2026-11-04 conditional approval supersedes the original final release question: all specified gates must pass before main merges, production transition and tags. Finish the feasible active tasks and final-tree checks, retain incomplete flow/response/platform scopes and report remaining issues. If any release condition remains unmet, ask once with its numbers and wait before release. First repository upload still requires REQ-107 confirmation; local tests do not provide it.

## Coverage

Historical checkpoint: Current 710-case inventory: blocked 4, not run 517, partial 80, pass 39, pass (live) 35, pass (mock/offline) 35. Partial execution and external prerequisites are counted separately. Earlier Workers performance failures remain historical; revised OVH verdicts are pending.

## Bugs

BUG-501 (TC-1269, SPEC-113/116): the temporary development module returned internal/500 while its default fetch was called with the verifier as receiver. A global-call wrapper fixed it; a host-receiver regression test and the real user/agent role trials pass. Fixed in this Access foundation bundle. This is a temporary-entrypoint failure, not evidence about unimplemented API operations. Preserve each failure with its TC/SPEC/SCR/API response, steps, expected/actual data effect, environment/commit, safe traceId and screenshot/API evidence. Impressions are recorded separately until the maintainer chooses to make them bugs/tasks.

## Notes for later stages

Each implementation PR updates only the cases it actually ran and cites raw evidence. A server contract pass means provided; accepted additionally requires the real-server screen/flow. Default/internal mock cases, manual Access trials, CI matrix and image review remain distinct. Keep the source first, then update/read back Notion.

## Access evidence (TASK-201)

Development Access on 2026-11-03 supplied ctx.access and the configured audience for both a real browser user and a service token. getIdentity could not distinguish them. The presence-only table and the verifier choice were written to stage 06 before authentication implementation. A pinned-team RS256/JWKS signature, issuer, audience and expiry check then verified both real assertion headers; no token, cookie, email or service client ID is retained.

The implementation was exercised through a temporary development entrypoint using the actual Access/Problem modules: user `/` 200, `/user-only` 200, `/agent-only` 403 agent_only; agent `/` 200, `/agent-only` 200, `/user-only` 403 user_only. Wrong audiences, forged/expired tokens, key rotation/outage, malformed assertions and missing/null sub use synthetic signed fixtures on Node 24.18.0, not altered real identities. Node tests are 29/29 on this branch (14 Access/Problem and 15 existing). A read-only code/security review found two issues in an earlier module version (unexpired-key cache lost on refresh failure and nonempty service name with missing/null sub); both were corrected and independently rechecked.

These are authentication foundation and temporary-entrypoint checks. app.js still uses its phase-1 routing: TASK-209 and TASK-210 remain in progress, and no API-101–API-132 response, page or flow is marked provided/accepted by this evidence.

Commit `51cc0d3` is the Access foundation tested above. The full Windows/Python 3.13 repository suite also passed 817 tests (14 skipped), and all four strict Claude plugin validations passed. The edited OpenAPI passes openapi-spec-validator 0.7.1. Full development API contracts and screens remain untested.

## Server bundle evidence (in progress)

Node 24.18.0 on Windows: 85 tests pass, including real synthetic RSA signatures, complete handler round trips, two distinct quota writers, same-ID/same-clock races, seven-day receipt expiry, claim expiry/renewal, batch rollback, migration 3 and checkpoint withdrawal racing a push. `entry-store.test.js` and `api-02.test.js` exercise the new transactions. openapi-spec-validator 0.7.1 passes. Full Windows/Python 3.13 checks pass: 817 tests, 14 skipped, 487.952 seconds; the four strict Claude validations, final Node 85/85 and OpenAPI validator pass. Independent code/security review and its fix follow-up have zero current actionable findings at the matching source hashes. The Python bridge initially failed because its local Access fixture still had no signed assertion; the fixture now signs with a synthetic RSA key and the four `test_nexus.py` tests pass.

Development deployment `085af289-f16b-4be0-b9cc-7db5514df11d` serves the uncommitted server implementation (184.24 KiB raw / 46.33 KiB gzip, startup 4 ms). A real service-token run made 16 requests against only `qa-nexus-api`: push, reads, comment/reply replay (one reply), proposals, user-only refusal, withdrawal, answered and dry run. It created synthetic entries only. Human-role browser-fetch checks, full Schemathesis contracts and screen flows are separate; no complete request task is called done here.

TC-1270 failed on the exact 1,000,000-byte, 3,000-item, 2,000-check, three-picture synthetic fixture: initial CPU 222/128 ms, optimized full repeat 83 ms; diagnostic body-only warm 18 ms and validation 73 ms. Worker Observability supplied CPU values; client wall time is not CPU. The optimized diagnostic deployment was `1af44788-e7e7-41d1-a68d-f4c5f33dd18b`. Business D1 calls were 2, containing 17 SQL statements; the repeated push wrote 1 logical snapshot row, the initial push 10 rows. The instrumentation wrapper and synthetic fixture are temporary; this is a failed free-plan trial, not full API contract evidence. A contract-splitting/paid-budget/further-optimization decision is pending with the maintainer; no plan, billing or frozen limits have changed.

BUG-502: maximum push exceeds the CPU target (TC-1270, REQ-124); open, awaiting direction. BUG-503: a checkpoint withdrawn between push planning and its transaction could lose the response warning; fixed by a checkpoint basis guard and one replan, with a deterministic SQLite race test.

The human Access browser-fetch smoke run also passed 10 checks on the synthetic project: acceptance created one pending reflection, dismissal created none, checkpoint pass/withdraw/read-back worked, human proposal was agent_only, both overview HEADs were empty and the localized missing-project browser page returned HTML 404. This is not a finished screen flow. Independent Aside code/security review reproduced two P2 issues (view replacement-string expansion and stale passed-checkpoint recorded_commit); both are fixed with regression tests and the follow-up reports zero current findings against matching source hashes. BUG-504/505 record these resolved defects.

### Review and bounded live contracts

PR kodonghui/corthex#93 at `7788177` passed the Linux/macOS/Windows checks, Worker dry build, four plugin validations and all-checks (run 41671690658). Its code review found three confirmed defects: a matching `replaces` could clear a held commit when the incoming commit was absent (BUG-506); a board proposal did not recognize `BoardTerm.term` (BUG-507); and comment details silently cut historical proposals at 500 (BUG-508). These are fixed with guarded no-mutation, board membership and 501-proposal lifetime regressions. The fourth suggestion would change frozen SPEC-120, which explicitly excludes `without_table`/`without_screen` terms from pairing; that behavior is preserved and separately tested, rather than changing the functional spec during a fix.

Schemathesis 3.39.16 with experimental OpenAPI 3.1 collected all 32 operations against development deployment `7469ecca-4c70-41ae-9a68-f778cefba3fe`, using only the synthetic `qa-nexus-contract` namespace and the service role. The bounded run (three generated examples per operation plus documented examples) passed 32 operations in 77.75 seconds; each of five checks passed 154/154 responses. This includes refusals and does not provide all 411 response examples or human-role success paths. It tests the pre-review-fix deployment, not the three new fixes.

The first run stopped on a test hook's absent path-parameter dictionary; that hook is corrected. The second reported two false schema failures: this pinned Schemathesis version always selects the first response media schema (HTML), even for a valid JSON Problem. `nexus/test/contract_hooks.py` adds `response_schema_by_media_type`, which selects the actual documented JSON media type and retains the tool's resolver and JSON Schema 2020-12 validation. `contract_hooks_check.py`, run only in the external pinned environment, accepts a valid Problem and rejects three invalid Problems. Content type, status, headers and server-error checks remain enabled. No contract was relaxed to make the run pass.

### Mobile editor trial (not accepted)

The same development deployment served pinned Excalidraw 0.18.1, ERD Editor 5.1.0 and React 18.3.1 as Worker modules, including fonts. Forty fresh Chrome mobile openings (20 per editor), viewport 390x844, DPR 2.75, touch, CPU slowdown 4x, disabled browser cache and bypassed service workers used DevTools Fast 4G: latency 165 ms, download 8.1 Mbit/s and upload 1.35 Mbit/s. Nearest-rank P95 was 2982.2 ms for Excalidraw and 3275.7 ms for ERD; all reported 300 elements, no exceptions or CSP violations. Thus TC-1271 fails (BUG-509). These are cold browser-cache openings; edge caches are not forcibly cold. Screenshots show the synthetic graph, and ERD fitting/column display still needs review. A completion marker alone is not final visual acceptance.

The split trial bundle contains about 23.33 MB of raw modules / 16.26 MB summed gzip, including 13,107,068 font bytes, and initial wire transfer was about 0.54 MB (Excalidraw) / 0.34 MB (ERD). Cloudflare's current official [Workers limits](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/limits/) allow 64 MiB uncompressed Worker code on both plans; the deployed prototype fits. Source files, complete measurements and screenshots are retained in the external trial workspace; product assets, licenses and reproducible packaging are not integrated yet. Earlier custom slow-network diagnostics and one-open trials remain diagnostics, not replacements for these 20-open results. The maintainer was offered continued optimization with D11 retained, a lighter ERD renderer, or a review of the timing requirement; no frozen decision has changed.

The review fixes were then redeployed to development version `0368a750-7eb7-4c33-b032-24e8aa2e74bd` (23,051.57 KiB raw / 15,805.17 KiB gzip including the trial editors, startup 6 ms). The same bounded five-check Schemathesis run passed all 32 operations and 154/154 responses per check in 59.59 seconds. Aside independently confirmed the existing, active exact-development-host Configuration Rule with only `disable_rum: true`, no injected beacon on a fresh page, unchanged frozen CSP and preserved Access; its verification made no account changes. The first full Python rerun had one public-safety failure caused by an external Hypothesis cache containing a local file path, not source material. The generated cache was moved intact to the external QA workspace, external tool storage was pinned there, and the public-safety checks passed; the complete suite is rerun before committing.

Final pre-commit rerun: Windows/Python 3.13 passed all 817 tests (14 skipped) in 398.031 seconds. Node 24.18.0 passed 89/89 tests, and all four strict Claude plugin validations passed. The external media-type check self-test passed as described above.

Independent follow-up review confirmed all three JavaScript fixes and the frozen SPEC-120 interpretation, but found two external QA-tool defects. BUG-510: the initial origin check alone did not prevent Requests from following a redirect with custom service headers. BUG-511: the default sanitizer omitted the service client identifier. The CLI process_call_kwargs hook now sets allow_redirects=False at the actual call boundary; live direct Case.call without that guard fails closed. Both authentication header names are configured in the loader and each runner thread's sanitizer. A real pinned CLI/transport run through an offline redirect adapter makes no credential-bearing second hop, and curl/CLI diagnostics contain neither synthetic authentication component. No real credentials were used by this reproducer or observed to leak. The earlier live runner also removed the actual environment values from its in-memory output before writing sanitized receipts. These fixes affect the external QA harness, not production Worker routing.

A subsequent GitHub code review found three further contract edges: BUG-512, the OpenAPI historical proposal array still had the old 500 cap; BUG-513, the valid part name constructor could resolve an inherited object property instead of a numeric unopened count; BUG-514, view bootstrap omitted its required generated_at. Stage 06 was clarified first to preserve API-114's existing all-history promise, and its historical array cap was removed. Overview lookup now uses own properties, push counts use a null-prototype map, and both page envelopes carry a UTC timestamp. Regressions cover an empty constructor count, 1,500 discrepancy candidates with the last 500 counted under that part, and the exact bootstrap fields/time. The external actual-handler corpus passes full JSON Schema 2020-12 checks for both extracted bootstraps, constructor overview and all 501 historical proposals. This is offline corpus proof, not new live response or screen acceptance.

The QA-guard follow-up also found stale admission state if a synthetic Case was reused after switching to live mode. The guard is now consumed once in every mode; a synthetic guarded call followed by direct live reuse is rejected before transport. The actual pinned CLI offline redirect/sanitizer controls and media-schema positive/negative controls pass. An earlier full repository rerun before these last Worker/schema regressions passed 817 tests (14 skipped) in 392.617 seconds; final-tree checks are rerun for this fix bundle.

BUG-515: a known space with no picture was incorrectly returned as a target-refusal response. API-102 already requires a working view shell whose data is null and whose problem explains picture_not_found. That route now preserves HTTP 200 for this empty state (including HEAD) while the JSON picture API remains 404 and unknown parts remain refused. The offline response corpus also validates this empty bootstrap. The new nexus-contracts CI job installs pinned tools in the runner's temporary directory and verifies OpenAPI, actual-handler response/bootstrap schemas, redirect/guard controls and failing two-worker redaction without a real account or network calls from the test adapters. TASK-248/249 are in progress; Prism mocks and complete named-response coverage remain unfinished.

The hook recheck reports zero current code/security findings at matching source hashes after its stale-mode fix, including a separate two-worker failing-diagnostic control. A committed parallel diagnostic regression also passes locally. Live bounded Schemathesis against development a656adf1-352d-4472-8939-0963c028ed63 passed 32 operations, five checks each 154/154, in67.98seconds before the last empty-view-shell correction. The full Python rerun passed817 tests(14skipped) in405.182seconds. The last shell correction is additionally checked through the actual handler and the full offline bootstrap schema corpus; final hosted checks cover the complete committed tree.

Final local correction checks: Node 92/92; the four Python NEXUS bridge/shared-file tests and two public-safety tests pass after the last shell change; all four strict Claude validations pass. OpenAPI0.7.1, full offline normal/empty bootstrap and501-history schemas, the real pinned CLI redirect/reuse controls, and deliberately failing two-worker redaction controls pass. The full817-test run above and these targeted final checks are distinct receipts; hosted CI must pass on the final commit before integration merge.

### Hub implementation checks (2026-11-04)

External Prism 5.16.0 on loopback was called once for each of the 411 named response/media examples, with valid synthetic path/header/body inputs and explicit Prefer code/example selection. All 411 matched status, media type, exact example body and full JSON Schema 2020-12; no request-schema or reported Prism violations remained. The first harness run incorrectly passed the literal default as a status; it was corrected to select each default example's actual 500/405 status, then the complete 411-call run passed in 3.984 seconds. This is mock body/status evidence only: common response headers, persisted effects, frontend states and all corresponding development outcomes are still checked separately, so it does not automatically pass the full TC rows or prove the server provided them. Tools and synthetic receipts remain outside product dependencies.

The 70 named default cases now have additional scoped evidence. `default_response_check.py` and `default-response-corpus.mjs` exercise the actual handler offline: 70/70 statuses/media, six common headers, exact CSP, Allow, the error heading, trace/log association and full response schemas pass. Internal/500 deliberately uses no database binding, so no state is touched; the 35 internal cases are mock/offline evidence. The 35 method-not-allowed examples were also requested as unsupported PATCH on development (32 JSON, three HTML): 35/35 passed in 18.109 seconds, with Allow, common/CSP headers, full JSON schema or HTML trace, and the same hexadecimal trace ID as the response's Cf-Ray. The response Ray adds a region suffix that the Worker input may lack; an initial full-string comparison was an extra harness assumption and was corrected without changing the contract. An earlier generic-User-Agent trial returned 403; the final run identifies the client as the actual hub does. Both earlier receipts are retained. Live console logs were not opened; log association was separately asserted through the actual offline handler. These passes do not imply all named responses or frontend acceptance.

The actual Python CLI completed all 18 distinct commands against development using only qa-hub-command-02, a synthetic Git repository. There were 28 invocations: 27 done and one preserved harness refusal from incorrectly supplying --commit with reflect merged; the documented form then passed. Aside performed six human-browser-fetch checks to accept its synthetic proposal, pass a synthetic checkpoint and read back their edit/checkpoint. Reflect/record opened/merged, queue drain and removal then succeeded; the entire synthetic space was removed. This is Windows CLI and real user/agent API proof, not a browser screen walk or the full three-OS outcome inventory.

Server PR kodonghui/corthex#93 was integrated as c5e7308 after workflow41684359822 passed every job, including the three OS suites, and final code/security review had no unresolved findings. This is foundation validation; the named live responses and screens remain separate work.

The hub boundary suite uses real synthetic Git object histories, repository records, loopback HTTP and subprocess lease contention. It exercises default-commit isolation, longest part prefixes, no filters/fsmonitor, optional unsafe-link omission, calendar dates, atomic part records, SQL numeric precision, nested safe YAML, fresh-push ancestry, whole-picture size omission, original-ID retries, seven-day expiry, stale-owner fencing, concurrent append ordering and text-free queue metadata. All eight agent writes cover done/refused/kept/invalid CLI and failure to persist. Whole-request deadlines cover a trickling body and DNS that finishes after cancellation; no late HTTP headers reach the test peer. Credentials in probes are synthetic.

The external pinned contract environment validates a real hub snapshot, written/raw repository records, the English/Korean NEXUS domain including all five timeline lanes, NEXUS ERD/sitemap and an OutboxFile against the full OpenAPI schemas. It also compares all copied server input schemas exactly. No repository snapshot is uploaded. The Python 3.8/latest six-cell OS matrix and final bundle review are required before integration; test-method totals are not accepted-case or live-response totals.

These failures were reproduced and corrected locally on the hub branch. Controls use synthetic repositories/responses and loopback peers, with no production traceId or observed real credential exposure. The outbox/transport/error controls also passed independent Python 3.8.20/3.13.14 probes. Exact-head CI/review and acceptance-case results remain separate.

| BUG-517 | SPEC-129, API-150 | Millisecond ties or a direct fresh send could overtake old writes. ID-only admission order and one shared prequeued drain pass tie/concurrent controls. |
| BUG-518 | SPEC-129, API-139–API-146 | Lease loss could lose the current original ID/body or overwrite a new owner's retry metadata. Durable-before-send, fencing and missing-only recovery preserve both owners' data. |

Redirected/reparse components and a resolved metadata location inside the repository are now refused without replacing the record. This required local control of the private guard tree. |

The opened guard's regular-file type, single link and named-object identity are checked before initialization, without truncating it. This required local control of the private guard tree. |

Top-level comma actions are now parsed separately; composite-column, quoted-comma, default/restrict/set-null and later-cascade controls retain both targets, rules and source lines. |
| BUG-534 | REQ-149, TC-1271 | Two isolated mobile openings received 403 on the core module while the page/editor/font requests succeeded. The final prototype therefore completed only 18/20 Excalidraw openings. Root cause is under read-only account/response investigation; no Access policy is relaxed and no successful P95 is inferred from the remaining openings. |

The PR kodonghui/corthex#97 follow-up independently verified BOM/selection, strike removal, and complete record serialization on actual Python 3.8.20 and 3.13.14. Its first pass found the timeline-endpoint, junction and hardlink residuals above. After their correction, a fresh hash-matched bounded rerun passed 20/20 methods per runtime with no remaining finding in that scope: actual NTFS junctions at all three metadata components, empty hardlinks unchanged, ordinary native guards, mixed-version/profile/path-alias writers, busy/retry reload and process-death release. POSIX FIFO behavior was unavailable on this Windows host and is not claimed from that review. Local supplied hub regressions passed 69/69 on Python 3.13; the complete Python 3.8 hub run collected 138, passed 137 and skipped one environment-specific control. The pre-ALTER-fix full-suite rerun at 2a6b5df collected 887, passed 873 and skipped 14 in 496.508 seconds; Node passed 93/93 and all four strict plugin validations passed. The external Git-object/picture/record/outbox schemas, 70 actual-handler default controls, response corpus and OpenAPI validator also passed. These are local implementation checks, not additional named acceptance-case passes or a finished browser flow.

### Later editor trial evidence (development only)

The unchanged earlier comparison artifact 95426489 was opened quietly 20 times per editor at each CPU setting, with the same 390x844/DPR2.75/touch/cache-off/Fast4G conditions. CPU 1x P95 was Excalidraw 1661.8 ms and ERD 1107.3 ms; additional 4x stress P95 was 2500.2/2448.1 ms. All four groups completed 20/20 with 300 elements and no observed CSP/page errors. REQ-149 names mobile/4G but no CPU slowdown factor; both settings and all older failures remain explicit. The ERD overview's clipped titles were then fitted without changing their text or D11; its column hiding at low zoom is the editor's deliberate level of detail, not proof of missing model columns.

A later packaging/label-fit prototype, development 791c3873-33c4-40b0-a670-362e4692f494, retained Excalidraw 0.18.1, ERD Editor 5.1.0 and React 18.3.1. It pins the optional Liberation fallback to unmodified OFL 2.1.5 with recorded source/conversion hashes. Its 442 Worker modules total 23,410,780 raw bytes / 16,333,610 summed gzip bytes. In the final 20-per-editor 4x/Fast4G run, ERD completed 20/20 with P95 2520.9 ms, but Excalidraw completed only 18/20: two core-module requests returned 403 while page/editor/font requests returned 200. Excalidraw has no valid P95 for that batch. The completed drawings held 300 elements; no CSP or uncaught page exception was observed, while the core did not run in the two failed openings. A later diagnostic-only ten browser openings and 100 direct core reads all returned successfully, which does not resolve the original intermittent failures or replace the failed acceptance batch. Read-only account/response diagnosis is pending (BUG-534). Packaging/notices and full screens remain unfinished; TC-1271 stays fail and no design/billing decision is inferred.

### Multi-action ALTER follow-up

BUG-533's fresh independent correction review passed 10/10 methods on each actual Python 3.8.20/3.13.14, with current input hashes matched. The old counterexample failed as expected; current controls preserved independent delete rules, composite/quoted commas, exact source lines and unchanged CREATE/single-ALTER output. The required current-tree full rerun then collected 888, passed 874 and skipped 14 in 514.484 seconds. Python 3.8 hub collected 139, passed 138 and skipped one in 84.209 seconds. Node 93/93, four strict validations and external hub schemas passed.

A separate diagnostic browser run reproduced BUG-534 with booleans confirming both service components matched on the failing code requests; responses were application/problem+json, without a captured problem code. Aside's read-only account review found 448 allowed and zero denied Access authentication entries in the original bounded window, and same-module Worker errors correlated with access_missing searches. This does not by itself bind the original responses to a verifier stage. No settings were changed. Failure-only, booleans-only development instrumentation is being used to isolate that stage; no real identity/assertion/component values are recorded, and the mobile gate remains unaccepted.

## Transport, measurement and release decision of 2026-11-04

The maintainer approved edge caching of public content-hashed code, styles and fonts at the same configured origin (REQ-147). HTML, API, project data and refusals remain no-store. Code has no Set-Cookie or Vary:*, with unchanged CSP and whole-host Access. Even a cached object must not reach unauthenticated GET/HEAD callers. An edge HIT does not visit the origin, so origin verification is not claimed on every HIT.

REQ-124 server processing runs from complete body receipt to response handling completion. Body arrival, client round trip, process CPU windows and RSS remain separate. The existing31.085ms passes1s; the historical inclusive1031.079ms stays recorded.

The maintainer conditionally approved NEXUS merges, production migration/routing and release tags after REQ-149/124 pass, all710 cases have verdicts with zero Must failures, migration/recovery rehearsals pass, core integrates after C1 release/PR C, and required checks plus Code/Security reviews pass. ## First picture decision, 2026-11-04

The maintainer explicitly selected “SVG first + HTML path improvement” in the current Codex conversation. Keep the frozen tokens, same-origin Access boundary and pinned real editors. A complete server-rendered SVG is the first picture; Excalidraw/ERD Viewer loads afterwards. REQ-149/TC-654/TC-1271/TC-1309 measure this first visible picture under the unchanged mobile/network/CPU1 profile. Record native-editor readiness separately, keeping all earlier native-ready failures and any failed/incomplete observations. Do not use a DOM-ready marker alone as evidence of a visible, readable picture.

The preview uses the same deterministic geometry and checked source as the editor, with safe text/fragment links and no SVG scripts, foreign content or external resources. It stays visible during editor loading and remains available with the item list when that loading fails. Core controls keep the current viewport when handing over to the real editor. Loading preserves the frame and surrounding layout. Distinguish first-preview and native-editor-ready states and timings.

Trusted static view CSS may be inlined so a stylesheet request does not block the preview. HTML, SVG project content and API data remain no-store; hashed code/style/fonts retain the approved Access-protected public immutable cache. The standalone server may negotiate bounded built-in HTML compression to reduce Tunnel transfer. No project data is placed in a public code file or cache. Neither this decision nor a preview is a release, first real-repository upload, or approval to delete Worker/D1. Existing conditional-release gates remain.
